  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:25:## The rule
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:26:
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:27:Every non-trivial fire (architecture audit / 
SOTA convergence / token-eff investigation / cross-model verification / gap-resolution wave) MUST spawn a 3-5 agent team. 
**Scope**: output fires only — rule-edit fires are governed by `cross-model-consensus.md` T1/T2/T3 gates alone, not this 
standing directive. **CADP cap**: concurrent dispatch is bounded by `parallel-agent-wave.md §CADP rule 2` (max 3 
concurrent unless `python Z:/claude/ccc/tools/status.py` shows cache ≥50% verified); 4-5 agents MUST use staggered 
dispatch — see §Anti-patterns for the full CADP carve-out. The team MUST satisfy ALL of:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:28:
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:29:1. **GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE for ≥2 agents** — 
`codex-rescue` / `gpt5-reviewer` / `gpt5-archaeologist` (Sonnet wrapper invoking real GPT-5.5 via codex CLI); satisfies 
cross-model-asymmetry per `cross-model-consensus.md`. **Per-call codex time-budget mandate** (Wave 44 FM-17.d 
codification, refined Wave 45 Fire 1 per codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.84 Pattern A): each codex call within agent brief 
MUST budget time per call -- **default 90s, normal cap 120s, 180s only with explicit reason**. Bounded, independently 
answerable calls preferred over one xhigh open-ended call when the question decomposes cleanly; keep one call when 
setup/context dominates OR the answer requires integrated judgment. Wrapper stream-watchdog 600s timeout fires before 
xhigh codex completion (empirical 5-15min per `cross-model-consensus.md §Profile selection rule`); short-call budget 
bypasses watchdog OR enables main-session foreground+tee fallback per `fm17-subagent-fleet-depletion.md §FM-17.d` recovery
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:30:2. **Brief cites SOTA repos at file:line 
depth** — every claim in the agent brief carries `Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/<file>:<line> @ HEAD <SHA>` OR 
`code.claude.com/docs/...` OR `mcp__github__get_file_contents` result; agent briefs ARE inputs to the agent's reasoning 
per `feedback_agent_team_brief_must_cite_sota_repos_at_file_line_2026_05_02.md`
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:31:3. **Line-by-line SOTA repo audit mandate** — 
for adoption-class waves, agents must Read the candidate repo's relevant `<file>` end-to-end (NOT skim README); Probe DAG 
1-7 per `agent-harness-fit-verification.md`
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:32:4. **Anthropic official docs as authority 
guide** — `code.claude.com/docs/en/*` is TIER-1 LIVING-AUTHORITY per `cross-model-consensus.md` Normative Authority Map; 
CCBP `Z:/repos/deps/claude-code-best-practice-shan/` HEAD-pinned is TIER-2 third-party reinforcement
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:33:5. **ARTIFACT-INLINE per FM-19** — Bash-only 
/ no-Write agents (architect / gpt5-reviewer / gpt5-archaeologist / code-reviewer) embed `## ARTIFACT-INLINE: 
tmp/wave<N>-<agent>-<topic>-<date>.md` in final return; orchestrator persists post-completion per 
`fm19-readonly-guard-sidestep.md`
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:34:6. **Mia pre-apply on returned 
prescriptions** — every prescription with `prescribed_edits` / specific file:line claims gets cheap-probe verification 
BEFORE Edit per `mia-pre-apply.md` (28/29 broad-fan-out prescriptions catch as OVER-claims pre-apply per Mia n=29 ladder)
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:35:7. **Forward-only persistence** — output 
paths follow `tmp/wave<N>-<agent>-<topic>-<date>.md` convention; orchestrator-side `mv` to canonical path (gitignored per 
`.gitignore:216`); reference memory files for cross-arc evidence
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:36:8. **OUTPUT_BUDGET and TERMINATION in every 
brief** — every agent brief MUST include `OUTPUT_BUDGET: <max LOC | max words>` (bounds artifact size for orchestrator 
ingestion per `team-orchestration.md §OUTPUT_BUDGET semantic`) AND `TERMINATION: on_handoff_to: orchestrator | max_turns: 
N | terminationCondition: <predicates>` per `team-orchestration.md §Termination contract`

# Long-running conventions for this project

## Always start here
Before doing anything else, read `PROGRESS.md`. It is your handoff note from the previous session. If it doesn't exist yet, create it now with four sections (`## Done`, `## In progress`, `## Next`, `## Notes`) and leave them empty. Then run `git log --oneline -10` to see what was just committed, and run the project's smoke test (or `npm run build` / `npm test`) once so you know you're starting from a working tree, not a broken handoff.

## One feature at a time
Work on exactly one item from `PROGRESS.md` per session. Finish it (tests passing, screenshot verified) before starting another. If the user gives you a new task mid-session, add it to `PROGRESS.md` and finish the current item first.

## Proof before passing
A test is only "passing" after you have:
1. Run it against the live app (Playwright screenshot or equivalent)
2. Opened the resulting screenshot or console log with the Read tool
3. Confirmed it shows what it should

The `verify-gate` hook will deny writes to `test-results.json` until you have opened evidence. Do not try to work around it.

## Keep `PROGRESS.md` current
After each completed item, update `PROGRESS.md`: check off what's done, add what you learned, note what's next. Future sessions read this file cold.

## Commit often
The `Stop` hook commits tracked changes at session end, but also `git add` new files and commit yourself at meaningful checkpoints with descriptive messages.

## If you're told to stop
`OPERATOR STEERING:` messages come from a human via the steer hook. Treat them as higher priority than your current plan.

You are reviewing work that a separate builder agent just claimed is complete. You did not see how it was built and you should not trust the builder's own assessment.

Do the following every time:

1. Read the spec or acceptance criteria for the feature under review.
2. Run `git diff` against the baseline to see exactly what changed.
3. Open every screenshot or console log under `screenshots/` (or wherever the builder was told to put evidence) and look at what they actually show, not what the filenames imply. If a file fails to open or returns an error, treat it as missing evidence.
4. Decide.

Plausibility is not correctness. A diff that looks reasonable paired with a screenshot that shows a broken layout is NEEDS_WORK. Missing evidence for any acceptance criterion is NEEDS_WORK. If you find yourself assuming something probably works, stop and look for proof.

Begin your reply with the bare word `PASS` or `NEEDS_WORK` on its own line, with nothing before it, so a wrapper script can read the verdict. Then:

- `PASS`: one line stating what evidence convinced you.
- `NEEDS_WORK`: a bullet list of specific, fixable findings the builder can act on next session.

Use Bash only for `git diff`, `git log`, and `ls`/`cat`. You cannot edit, write, or run the application. Do not offer to fix anything yourself.
